# Oteryn Game / Canary parity inventory — 2026-10-25

Status: research inventory, not architecture acceptance or implementation authority. Task: `PARITY-INVENTORY-1`. Server behaviour only; no client rendering comparison. Oteryn snapshot: `865c9231061f54b15063d20c1bf7ced3c3dd7d02` (working tree initially clean). Primary reference: Canary `47dfd51f45280a59a1d3e50ba7edd573d7234446`; secondary: Crystal Server `ff7ede593c69d4c658b382c97443e8155926924a`. Reference directories have no Git metadata; revision identity is supplied by the task, with examined-file SHA-256s below.

## Method, status semantics and limits

Tables are generated by `python3 -I scratch-parityinv/extract.py` and `python3 -I scratch-parityinv/build_report.py`. Both scripts and their machine-readable extraction are local, untracked research scratch. They read the two reference roots and this worktree; they never execute donor scripts. XML extraction uses Expat with original line numbers, expands inclusive id ranges, unions ids per exact case-sensitive key, includes nested attributes, and excludes XML comments. Multiple occurrences on one id count once. Item-level `fromid`/`toid` counts count every id in that range. Counts are exposure populations, not Oteryn coverage percentages.

C++ extraction balances function braces with string/comment awareness, extracts every case in `parsePacketFromDispatcher`, the viewer switch and direct dead/disconnected branches, then groups by opcode value. The packet-site count is switch/explicit-state branch sites, not invocation frequency; callee comparisons also inspect the parser implementation. OTBM counts are read-branch sites, not map placements (no binary map census). Return messages include shared-case aliases and the default string. Lua files under every `data/scripts/**` and `data-*/scripts/**` are partitioned once by mechanic/path; registration factory counts are lexical evidence and do not prove a script is enabled by server configuration. Unregistered helpers, spells, callbacks and migrations remain in the partition, rather than disappearing.

`PRESENT` means a cited native production route performs the stated narrow behaviour; it never promises source-wide equivalence. `PARTIAL` means a real subset, an unqualified data population, or unproven wiring/semantic equivalence, with a reason. `DATA-ONLY` means stored/imported/encoded metadata or a domain/storage API with no traced live command consumer. `MISSING` means no matching server command/field/consumer was located in the examined active registry and production sources; it is not a claim that the term never appears in tests or candidate code. Negative searches covered `apps/`, `crates/`, `tools/`, especially Content, promotion, native item imports and the live mechanic registry. Candidate spell casters and pure domain APIs are not credited as production.

Crystal `present` is an observed counterpart, not an equality assertion. XML compares values per common id and reports population additions/removals; nested child parameter differences have their own key rows. `differs` in C++/Lua means executable source differs after whitespace/comments are removed; the cited source and local difference ledger retain both sides. Representative changed statements are shown, but this is not a formal proof of all branch/distance/limit equivalence. Untraced behaviour remains `PARTIAL`; Canary is the default comparison baseline, subject to accepted owner decisions. Same-name functions or same message text are never sufficient proof of parity.

## 1. Item XML keys and runtime consumers

Case variants are deliberately separate rows. `Context` is the parent key path: a spell name beneath `augments`, or an imbuement family beneath `imbuementslot`, is not a standalone Item field. Schema citations name destinations; only the final column credits execution. Content promotion affects qualified subsets and may deliberately leave Unknown/Conflict/NotApplicable; a schema destination does not prove that all counted source ids are populated.

### Document-field finding

The five logical keys `readable`, `writeable`, `allowdistread`, `maxtextlen`, `writeonceitemid` (including case aliases) are **DATA-ONLY for general books/signs**. Typed storage and promotion exist. A narrower mail implementation reads readable/writeable/max length and requires write-once NotApplicable: `crates/gameplay/durability/src/durability/mail_text_policy.rs:44`. However, searching all production callers of `mail_text_policy` and the item text writer finds no live dispatch call. Its presence is therefore not credited as runtime reading/writing. The active route fragments have no document command, and USE handles containers/consumables/runes/map mechanics rather than a text window. Reading a definition description with LOOK is separate from reading placed text.

## 2. OTBM, instance attributes and tile facts

Canary has separate serialized `AttrTypes_t`, map-header `OTBM_ATTR_*`, and in-memory `ItemAttribute_t` enums; equal-looking names are not interchangeable numeric wire ids. The table includes every read case found in `iomap*`, `Item::readAttr` and relevant derived readers. Some base-class branches only skip derived attributes; container-items is rejected by the base class and handled by Container. Oteryn imports B3 project sectors; it does not load OTBM in the serving process. The shared sector carrier is `crates/world-bundle/src/sector.rs:25`, compiler `tools/world-bundle-compiler/src/compile.rs:1258`, and runtime placement fact owner `crates/gameplay/world/src/map/facts.rs:74`.

## 3. Client → server packets

The pinned Canary entry point now delegates to `parsePacketFromDispatcher`; enumerating only the literal `parsePacket` body would miss the main switch. Every opcode below includes special viewer/dead-state branches. Native command **type ids** are compared by meaning, never by numeric equality with Tibia opcodes; per-session command sequence ids are a different concept. Native execution is gated by capability, generation and admission at `crates/gameplay/dispatch/src/gameplay_transport/connection/serve/command.rs:172`. The generated route include is mounted at `crates/gameplay/dispatch/src/gameplay_transport/connection/serve/route.rs:140`.

### Recvbyte modules reached before the C++ switch

The XML module registry is part of the actual `parsePacket` path. It can own a byte before the dispatcher, depending on protocol profile. In particular **Canary already handles `0x5F` through the Task Board module**; Crystal handling it in C++ does not make this a Crystal-only opcode. The union count above includes these values without double-counting switch values. Module script bodies are outside the requested `data*/scripts/**` census but are included here as packet consumers.

## 4. Scripts by mechanic

A disjoint partition of all Lua files in the requested roots follows. `Files` is the actual file count across data packs, including duplicate relative paths. Factories count files containing Action, MoveEvent, TalkAction, CreatureEvent or GlobalEvent after comments are removed; a multi-factory file contributes to more than one factory count. Support includes spells, event callbacks and helpers outside those five factory types. Quest directories are kept as individual mechanic groups, avoiding a single unreviewable “quests” bucket. Tools and door subtypes are separate. Crystal comparison joins relative `scripts/...` paths within paired packs (`data` ↔ `data`, `data-otservbr-global` ↔ `data-global`, `data-canary` ↔ `data-crystal`); duplicated pack copies are counted, not collapsed out of totals.

Every group with changed shared files is listed. Each row gives both source citations and one changed-statement example; the complete local line-difference ledger is `scratch-parityinv/source-differences.json`. A changed line can be a message, numeric limit, condition, or new branch. This ledger is evidence for a later mechanic task, not permission to adopt Crystal semantics. Source statements are quoted only in short illustrative excerpts.

## 5. Player-facing return values and messages

Every case label and returned string in `getReturnMessage`, including shared-case aliases and DEFAULT, is listed. Oteryn speaks `protocol-oteryn`: a typed refusal can represent the same condition without sending the reference English string. `PRESENT (typed)` below means the server produces that condition through a live route; exact text equivalence is not claimed. `PARTIAL` identifies folded or narrower native outcomes. A mere enum declaration is never credited. Counts are lexical references in reference `src/**/*.cpp` outside `getReturnMessage`, plus directly recognized `return`/`sendCancelMessage` uses; they are not dynamic calls and can include tests only if located under src (none of the extraction searches external test trees).

## 7. Ranked gap backlog (server only)

Ranking is DERIVED from ordinary player exposure (movement, item use, inventory, combat, progression), not telemetry. S/M/L estimates are planning guesses for a coherent mechanic batch. “Migration” distinguishes content regeneration from durable database changes; no migration is authorized here. Each row names source evidence as well as the active native gap. Existing accepted contracts and owner decisions must be checked before allocating any proposal.

## Validation and research limits

Local checks for this docs-only change: `git diff --check`; `python3 -I tools/agents/validate_governance.py`; independent XML/table/citation/partition assertions in `python3 -I scratch-parityinv/verify_report.py`. The routing policy for “other docs” requires `git diff --check`; governance is additionally run because the task requests it. Read-only searches of workflow/tool commands found no repository markdownlint/remark/Prettier docs lint invocation and no reviews index in this directory, so no index edit is needed. Check results and exact commands are recorded in the untracked root `PARITY-NOTES.md`.

Cargo test filters: **none (not applicable: documentation/research only)**. No Rust, schema, migrations, pins, tests, workflows, vendor files or runtime registrations changed. Consequently no compilation/clippy/PG claim is made. No new enum variants, struct fields, trait methods or public functions were added. The requested self-review checklist was reread; code-only checklist items are inapplicable.

Limits: static evidence only, no donor runtime or Oteryn gameplay session executed; no binary OTBM placement census; no proof of which alternative donor data pack is enabled in deployment. Source pin identity is task-provided (the reference exports lack `.git`). Factory/path classification and negative routing searches are reproducible but do not prove semantic absence outside the listed surfaces. Messages and numeric literal deltas cannot establish arbitrary condition/branch equivalence. Qualified native content populations were not assumed equal to either whole donor population. Proposed backlog sizing/migrations/dependencies are DERIVED planning estimates requiring owning-contract review; no issues were created.
